CREDffiLE WITNESS REPORT- 17 OCT 02

Reference:

A.     Your Loose Minute dated 21 Oct 02.

1.      At Reference, you asked whether the credible witness report submitted by 2 police officers
from Humberside represented anything of air defence interest. Following enquiries through HQ 2
Gp and RAF Neatishead, there is no evidence that unauthorised military activity or any other
activity of air defence interest occurred on 17 Oct 02 in the Hull area.

     Sighting Of Unidentified Flying Object

     Humberside Police Incident Log Number 70 of 17/10/02 timed at 0239hrs Thursday
     17I 10/02 refers

     At 0239 hrs on Thursday 17th October 2002 a member of the public contacted
     Humberside Police to report seeing a UFO. The adult male caller wished to remain
     anonymous but it is believed he lives in Hull (eastern area of the City). Whilst making the
     report the caller stated he could see two lit objects in the sky - one over the the Eastern
     Cemetary, Preston Road, Hull and the other one over B.P. Saltend. He stated the objects
     appeared to be 'communicating' with each other. He described one of the objects as
     having a blue light in the middle, a white light and two green lights with a red light going
     around the top. The object was spinning around and hovering.The caller was looking
     through binoculars.

     Local Police Officers were alerted and sighted what they believed to be the object being
     reported by the caller. The below report is from two Police Officers rather than from the
     initial caller who was reluctant to become involved. Other officers also saw the 'object'.

     a) Police Officers involved- P C - n d Pc
     b) 0254 hrs 17/10/02- duration ~utes
     c) Appeared as the size of a 'normal' bright star but of different colours- blue, green, red
     and white
     d) Viewed from various locations in the Hedon area (east of Hull) from a police vehicle
     e) Viewed with naked eye
     f) First viewed by officers to be in the sky over B.P. Chemicals Plant at Saltend near Hull
     and then in the Thorgumbald area.
     g) Angular elevation of approx 45 degrees
     h Distance from observers is difficult to estimate but not near to them
    · ~       object appeared to move fast and then stop - it stopped and            on a couple of
      pccasions and repeatedly changed its colour/shape, fading and also becoming brighter at
•     times
      j) Meteorological conditions - weather fine and dry with no cloud initially and no other
      objects nearby to obstruct the view.
      k) A chemical plant is nearby and also the River Humber with industrial/chemical plants
      on the south bank at Killingholme/lmmingham
      I) The Humberside Police Helicopter attended the area and perceived the object to be a
      star or satellite and the colour changes being an optical effect due to possible gasses
      emmitted from the nearby industrial plants. It has to be said however that the above
      officers were not convinced of this explanation.


      This report is submitted for your information and attention. If further details or clarification
      are required please contact the Humberside Police Northern Command Centre

              I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
      19 September 2002, the details ofwhich were passed this office by West Yorkshire Police. We are
      the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

              First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
      'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
      defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
      might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
      potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
      to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
      explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
      function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
      expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
     files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 19 September from
     Yorkshire. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
     Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

            Thank you for your letter of 27th September concerning an 'unidentified flying object' you saw on
            23 September.

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
            'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
            defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
            might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
            a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source, and to date no 'UFO'
            report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
            reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
            natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this
            kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public funds on
            investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report files
            and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 23 September from
            anyone in London. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the
            United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.


                                                 Yours sincerely,
                                                         2ih September 2002

Dear Sir/Madam,

23rd September 2002, at 1am I was talking to a friend on the computer, I have a
solid wood and leather chair. I felt vibrations and thought I imagined it, but a
second wave through the chair and stronger ones frightened me so much so that
I jumped out of it and to the other side of the room.

I got up and went out onto my balcony (I have french windows in this room), the
sky was exceptionally clear, it was very cold and there was only the faint hint of a
breeze. To give an idea of direction, the full moon was facing me slightly to the
right and Heathrow is to the right.

I looked to the right and saw a clear light about the size of a headlight about
pencil length above the rooftops of 4 story flats in that direction (I'm on the 5th
floor and about the same level as their roof) The glare was bright and I'm very
used to watching planes and helicopters being in central London.

I thought for a moment it was a plane but at the size it was, I would have
expected it to move or change direction or something or at the very least, grow in
shape if it was coming directly towards me for example, but it was stationary for a
good 5 minutes.

I ran in and prayed my batteries were okay and hunted for my digicam (nikon
coolpix 775) and ran back outside. I took a couple of pictures but it was very cold
out and I was shivering so they are a little blurred, but I also managed to get a
couple of short videos though again they're a little shaky, as the camera has a 15
second silent video feature.

Had that been a plane it would have grown in size and neared except that wasn't
the right direction for the usual flight path either.

A helicopter appeared from behind me and hung around between the full moon
and me or thereabouts and still the light didn't move straight away. Eventually it
moved slowly up and to the right in direction and I feared I would not be able to
see it as it slowly passed behind my flats. I ran to the other side of the flat and
out to the front balcony. First the helicopter came over the roof, (the whole time
there was only ever the helicopter noise, nothing at all from the object itself.) It
climbed and began to come right over head where now, although it was dark as
was the object (which did at that point look possibly like it could have been a
plane) when I realised the pattern of red lights were alternating and going in a
triangle shape, very clear bright flashing red lights in a continuous pattern, I could
make out a basic triangle shape and confirm to myself that this was not a normal
plane. The object moved in front of me from left to right and I managed to get a
photo of this. (Heathrow would now have been to my left, and the moon would
have been behind me).

Having loaded the photos to the computer it looks very odd. One light shows
only (they had alternated) and two of the outside edges can be seen though they
are blurry and look almost cloud like except there were no clouds whatsoever
and it stayed this shape the whole two-three minutes it took for it to pass over
heading towards the direction of Hampstead (if you imagine the point being close
to me and the flat side moving away) I was asked if it looked like a vapour trail
but this would have been completely reversed and was moving clearly (between
the size of a penny and a two pence piece) in the totally wrong direction for it to
have been so. It didn't look like that to the eye, to the naked eye it was a triangle
shape with red flashing/alternating lights that went round the inside edges of the
triangle shape and one in the centre. Just one edge red light showed up on the
photo though and I'm glad to have got as much as I did considering by now I was
absolutely trembling with the cold and trying to get the object in the picture at all.

I'm very used to planes, (though not camera's) I often watch and am used to
seeing anything up to 18 in the air at one time from my view and am also used to
seeing the Presidents aircraft landing in the American Ambassador's home in
Regents Park directly opposite me.

It was not a plane, it was not a helicopter, it most certainly wasn't a meteor (as
has also been suggested) as the shaky photo's can only show a split second still
and nothing like what the naked eye sees by way of clear lights flashing in a
continuous sequence and a definite shape etc.

From the tremors with the chair to the object leaving, the whole
thing lasted 16 minutes.

I would be very grateful for your input on this, including how many other reports
of the sighting there were. I have included a print out of the one of the
photographs.

Yours sincerely,

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    23 September 2002, which was passed to us by RAF Fylingdales. This office is the focal point
    within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

             First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the

             of the Cannock and Rugeley Mercury first contacted the low flying

     focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
     a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source, and to date no 'UFO'
     report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
     reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
     natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this

Dear

I am writing with reference to the report you made to RAF Leuchars of an 'unidentified flying
object', seen on I 0 September. The details have been passed to this office because we are the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source, and to date no 'UFO'
report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this
kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public funds on
investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm that we received one other report for
I 0 September. This involved three objects each of which had two bright orange lights in a
triangular formation and was seen at Hamilton, at 23.23. We are, however, satisfied that there is
no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by
unauthorised military aircraft.

Dear

I am writing with reference to the report you made to Glasgow Airport of an 'unidentified flying
object', seen on 10 September. The details have been passed to this office because we are the
focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source, and to date no 'UFO'
report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this
kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public funds on
investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

With regard to your particular observation, I can confirm that we received one other report for
10 September. This involved a small, bright, oval shaped object, seen at 19.45, in Perth. We are,
however, satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
airspace was breached by unauthorised military aircraft.

Dear Sir,

UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT REPORT

These are the particulars of a report received by ATC at Glasgow Airport on the 1oth September
2002 at 2340 local time.

A.       Sighting took place on 10/09/02 at 2323 hrs local and lasted for approximately 10 seconds.

B.       There were 3 objects each of which had 2 bright orange lights, in a triangular formation and
         making no sound.

C.       The observer was standing in the rear garden of his home in Hamilton observing the night
         sky.

D.       The objects were observed with the naked eye.

E.       The objects were first seen to the North of the observer, a bearing having been taken with
         a compass.

F.       No angular elevation available. Although difficult because of darkness observer estimated
         height to be 20 OOOft.

G.       Distance from observer not known.

H.       Objects tracked from North to South at high speed in a triangular formation but appeared to
         swap places with each other a number of times.

J.       Observer reported a clear sky with good visibility. Met. Conditions at the airfield reported as
         CAVOK.

M.       Observers details;


                                            Scotland

0.       Observer was sole witness to events.
                                        1
P.       Report received on 10 h September 2002 at 2340hrs local time.

       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
12 August 2002. The report was passed to this office by RAF Fylingdales as we are the focal
point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 12 August from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
13 August. The report was passed to this office by RAF Northolt as we are the focal point within
the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry ofDefence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explana.,tions, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 13 August from
Middlesex. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
3 June 2002, which was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service (Military) at
RAF West Drayton. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry ofDefence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 3 June from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

        I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
13 May 2002. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
(Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 13 May from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

              I am writing with reference to your e-mail dated 5 April 2002, concerning a sighting of an
     'unidenified flying object' in Ashford in Kent on 21 September 2001. Your e-mail was passed to
     this office as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to
     'UFOs.'

             First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry ofDefence examines any reports of
     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
     potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
     to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
     explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
     function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
     expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
     files and can confirm that we received one other report of a 'UFO' from Ashford on that date.
     However, I should wish to assure you that the integrity of the UK's airspace in peacetime is
     maintained through continuous surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force,
     and the MOD remains vigilant for any potential threat.          We are satisfied that there is no
     corroborating evidence to suggest that, on 21 September 2001, the United Kingdom's airspace
     was breached by unauthorised air activity. I hope this is helpful.

    Subject: ufo sightings
    Dear Sir/Madam.

    I am making investigations into a sighting on 21st September 2001 between 0400-0650,
    at Ham street on the (A2070) and was seen on the right hand side heading towards Ashford (Kent), Then the
    ship/craft moved towards Ashford and hovered over Ashford for some time, this is not the first time this type of
    craft has been seen.
    I do have more information on the sighting at this Date but
    I woutd like your help if you could provide information as to the height recorded by the radar, was the air
    defence compromised at this time on this Date in Kent or not.
    I know that the sighting was reported to the police and an inquiry made to other people, some of the other
    material evidence does show that the craft was solid and being controlled logically also positive radar tracking
    on the craft.
    I do hope you can provide some help to ascertain the ID or reason why this craft if not one of our own, should
    be able stay in British air space at low altitude with no warning to the danger to the public which could have
    easily happened if any malfunctions had occurred on the craft.
    As British air space is monitored it does concern us and others as to why the public should be put at risk.
    The risk is major factor not only in it being there in the first place But also what it is or was doing,

    I hope that you can provide a solution to this occurrence as we would like to find an answer too and I am sure
    that we are able to understand your offer to help.

    I trust that this well go to the right department and the people that it well concern are aware, that we are
    knowledgeable and interested in truth and not to be fooled by past answers which are not always positively or
    politically correct,

    We look forward to hearing from you and at this point also whish to thank you for reading our Email.

    Many Thanks.

        8 April 2002. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
        (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
        correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
        files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 8 April from Wales.
        We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United Kingdom's
        airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

        I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
25 March 2002. The report was passed to this office by RAF Leuchars as we are the focal point
within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful ifi explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 25 March from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

             'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
             defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
             might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
             potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
             to identity the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believ~ it is possible that rational
             explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
             function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
             expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                      With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
              files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 13 February from
              anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
              suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

          TREAT OFFICIAL CORRESPONDENCE

  To      ~M                              RefNo      \SSS          '/2002..
       --~~---------------


         The Secretary of State I                     has received the
   attached letter from a member of the public. This office has not
   acknowledged it.

         Please send a reply on behalf of the Minister. All Ministers attach
   importance to such letters being answered promptly; your reply sho·uld
   therefore be sent within 20 working days of receipt in this branch. If,
   exceptionally, this should prove impossible an interim reply should be
   sent within the same timescale.

         A new Open Government Code of Practice on Access to
   Government Information came into force in January 1997. All replies to
   members of the public must be in accordance with the procedures set out
   in the Code. A full explanation of the Code of Practice is contained in
   DCI(Gen) 223/99, further information is available from DG Info on
   extension

         Under the Citizens' Charter, Departments are now required to keep
   records of their performance. All branches and Agencies are required to
   keep information on the number of requests for information which
   refer to the Code of Practice including details of the correspondent and
   the nature and date of the reply. In addition, the Department is required
   to provide a record of the total number of letters from members of the
   public and provide statistics (which may be used on a valid sample) of
   its performance in providing replies within their published targets.

        As part of our monitoring procedure, random spot checks on
   the accuracy of your branch records on correspondence will be
   performed throughout the year.

             MINIST
         ROOM222 WH
                               10 DOWNING STREET
                                      LONDON SW1A2AA


  THE PRIME MINISTER                                                   6 November 2001


         The way we as a Government deal with communications from the public
  reflects our attitude towards those contacting us. I regard the letters, telephone calls
  and e-mails that we receive as being extremely important. They give a useful
  insight into the way the public thinks. Too often correspondence is seen as the poor
  relation and although over the last few years we have made good progress in the
  way Departments handle all forms of correspondence, I believe there is still more
  that can be done.


        Staff in all Departments continue to do a fantastic job coping with the vast
  amount of correspondence received each year. Ministers too need to ensure that
 they are doing their bit by supporting staff, being clear about their requirements and
 91ilking certain that they sign off letters on time.


        I am copying this to all Government Ministers, Sir Richard Wilson and
 Permanent Secretaries. Should you or your staff wish to discuss this please contact
~ere at No. 10.


 The Rt Hon John Prescott MP

                         From:

                                                                         D/DAS/64/2 .__
                                                                         Date
                                                                        26 March 2002


                I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
        7 March 2002. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
        (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
        correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                First, it may be helpful ifl explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
        'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
        defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
        might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
        potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
        to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
        explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
        function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
        expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

                With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
        files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 7 March from
        anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
        suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                                                               Your Reference
                                                               OtU:Reference . ../
                                                               D/UAS/64/2 V

                                                               Date I , March 2002


        I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object'
on 5 March 2002, the details of which were left on the Air Staff (LA) Operations and Policy 1
answerphone. This office is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence
relating to 'UFOs.'

       First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
a potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not
the function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 5 March from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                                                                Your Reference
                                                                 OtJL Referencv
                                                                 D/UAS/64/2
                                                                Date \, March 2002


        I am writing with reference to your recent letter and the enclosed video footage of lights in
the sky never Leven.

        As you will be aware from my letter of 18 December 2001, the Ministry ofDefence has
only a very limited interest in these matters and examines any reports of 'unidentified flying
objects' solely to establish whether what was seen might have some defence significance. I
should add that the integrity of the UK' s airspace in peacetime is maintained through continuous
surveillance of the UK Air Policing Area by the Royal Air Force. This is achieved by using a
combination of civil and military radar installations, which provide a continuous real-time
"picture" of the UK airspace. Any threat to the UK Air Defence Region would be handled in the
light of the particular circumstances at the time (it might if deemed appropriate, involve the
scrambling or diversion of air defence aircraft). From the perspective, reports provided to us of
'UFO' sightings are examined and we consult air defence staff and others as necessary. Only a
handful of reports in recent years have warranted further investigation and none revealed any
evidence of a threat.

       With regards to your video camera tape, please be assured that it was viewed by an air
defence expert who was satisfied that it contained nothing of defence interest. I return it to you
as requested.

              Dazzling Flying Triangle ~
           Visits South Coast Of England
                                                F

                                                    3-30-2

                 De~
                 Sighting on the 28th Februruy 2002 Time 22.50hrs
                 The size of the "flying triangle" was approximately 1OOfeet from the base of
                 the triangle to the apex. The size as was the overall description was
                 consistent with all witnesses that night. It had red lights running along the
                 length of the right hand side of the craft and blue along the left side.
                 A large circular light in the middle of the craft that pulsated in colour from a
                 pale neon blue through to purple. There were no reports of any noise only a
                 sound which was described as an electric crackle, similar to being
                 underneath a power pylon when it is wet.
                 First Report
                 The "craft was first seen at St. Austell in Cornwall by a hospital doctor out
               . walking his dog. Within minutes it was seen by several other people as it
                 hovered approximately 200 yards off the shore at 1,000 ft high. It's speed
                 increased from stationruy to 500 mph for 10 miles before doing a right hand
                 turn (sharp 90 degree) without slowing down. (report by pilot)
                 The next report I received was from Somerset from a couple who at first did
                 not see anything but were drawn to look upwards by an electric crackling
                 noise. They could not believe their eyes when they saw the craft hovering
                 above their heads estimated at about 2 to 3, 000 feet, the centre light rapidly
                 pulsating. It hovered for about two minutes maximum before "shooting off'
                 at a tremendous speed in a easterly direction.
                 Second Report
                 22.5 5 hours from Lyme Regis, Dorset by a retired Coast Guardsman. His
                 words: "The craft was hovering over the town, no more than 800 feet I
                 would estimate. At first I thought it was a new prototype aircraft but was
                 mystified by no sound and the unusual lighting display. I witnessed it for
                 only a minute at the most before it gained tremendous speed, no noise, the
                 light underneath pulsating a very pale blue with a corona around it. It
                 climbed vertically in a matter of seconds, it reminded me of a Harrier jump

        file://c: \windows\TEMP\triLNLIB .htm                                                   31/03/02
                                                                  -~AS
                                                                      08 APR   2fi02---·
                                                                 FilE=
                                                                                      -
                                                                                         Yage L. or L.


•           jet, I would estimate to a height of 2, 000 feet before it headed in a South
            Easterly direction."
            Third Report
            From Portland 23.00 hours, Dorset. Policeman and two other witnesses
            (statements all describe the craft identically). Once again hovering, over a
            set of transmitting aerials at approximately 1 to 2,000 feet. Totally
            noiseless, only hovered for a minute or two at the most. The "electric noise"
            was heard but two witnesses stated they were not sure ifit could have been
            the military transmitting aerials. Moved off slowly, almost at a hover speed.
            Fourth Report
            From Hengistbmy Head area, Dorset. 23.05 hours. Four separate witnesses
            walking and walking their dogs. Height estimated at about 500 to 600 feet
            over the sea, this description of height was confirmed by a fisherman out
            "dropping his crab pots". Once again, no noise reported, the navigation
            lights?? were pulsating like a "rope light." Only seen for a maximum of two
            minutes by witnesses. The craft departed this time by climbing vertically
            very quickly until it became about the size of a star and then "blinked out"
            the witnesses stated. The interesting thing at this location was that very soon
            after the craft started to ascend two jet fighters screamed overhead the
            witnesses said. There were a total of 18 witnesses that saw the craft during
            its flight path over the South Coast.

                       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
               24 February 2002. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
               (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
               correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

                       First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
               'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
               defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
               might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
               potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
               to identifY the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
               explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    19 January 2002. The report was passed to this office by RAF Northolt as we are the focal point
    within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 19 January 2002
    from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
    suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a

explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the

             I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
     I January 2002. The report was passed to this office by RAF Leuchars as we are the focal point
     within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

             First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
     'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
     defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
     might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justifY
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 1 January 2002 from
    anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
    suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

      NOTES:

      1.     Members of the public who submit reports should be informed that the information has been passed
     to the Ministry of Defence.

     2.      Sightings by Service personnel and action taken as a result of civilian sightings must not be
     disclosed to members of the Press who. if they make enquiries, should be referred to the Ministry of
     Defence.

     3.       A routine unclassified signal. or Fa~hould be sent to the Ministry or Defence, London
     (for the attention of MOD Sec(AS)2A). The format of the signal is as set out in this report proforma. The
     signal SIC is z6r

            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    30 December 2001. The report was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
    (Military) at RAF West Drayton as we are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
    correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 30 December 2001
from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

           I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    18 December 2001, the details of which were left on the Air Staff (Operations and Policy)
    answerphone. This is the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating
    to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 18 December 2001
    from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
    suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

             With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
     files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 10 December from
     anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
     suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                                                                 Pontefract

        Dear Sir/Madam,

         On the 26th of November 2001 a man and his wife witnessed a
        large orange ball hovering over South Elmsall, which is near
        Pontefract, West Yorkshire. The time he gave me was between
        7.30pm and 8.00pm. He could not give me an exact time, as he
        was not wearing his watch. From his position, which was near
        Hooton Pagnal, which over looks South Elmsall, he said that he
        stopped his vehicle and watched this thing through binoculars
        for twenty minutes.
        He told me that he could not see any more detail through the
        binoculars except that it was very large and brighter than a
        streetlight.
         He then carried on to South Elmsall where he lives. When he
        arrived at home he saw it again quite close to his position above
        South Elmsall. He then observed it a further minute or so and
        then it moved away at high speed to the north. No noise was
        heard from the object. Cloud cover was a quarter. He says that
        he could see other people watching it from the street.

        Could you please tell me if you received any reports of anything
        similar on that date?

        Could you possibly send me details of any reports of unusual
        objects seen that day?

          t .    I   -   .

         He then carried on to South Elmsall where he lives. When he

    Thank you for your recent letter addressed to my co ..................               concerning a bright light
    you saw in the sky over Tipton.

    It may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of sightings of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of
    a potential threat to the United Kingdom from an external military source, and to date no 'UFO'
    report has revealed such evidence, we do not attempt to identify the precise nature of each
    reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational explanations, such as aircraft lights or
    natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the function of the MOD to provide this
    kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify expenditure of public funds on
    investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit. As a result we are unable to assist
    with your particular query.

                                                Yours sincerely,

       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
9 December 2001, which was passed to us by RAF Leu chars. This office is the focal point within
the Ministry of Defence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 9 December from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

    can confirm that we received no 'UFO' sighting reports for this date from anywhere in the United
    Kingdom. We are satisfied that on this date there is no corroborating evidence to suggest the
    United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity, which, as you will be aware
    from previous correspondence, is our only interest in these matters.

            Thank you for your letter dated 14 November, concerning a sighting of an 'unidentified
    flying object' near Stillington on 10 November 2001. Your letters to RAF Leeming and RAF
    Fylingdales have also been passed to us for reply.

            I can confirm that we received no sighting reports of 'UFOs' from anywhere in the United
    Kingdom on 10 November. I can also confirm that there were no major military exercises taking
    place in or around the area on that date. I hope this is helpful.
•                    From:            Directorate of Air Staff (Lower Airspace)
                     Operations and Policy 1a, Room 6/73
                     MINISTRY OF DEFENCE
                     Metropole Building, Northumberland House, London, WC2N 5BP

                                                                     Your Reference

                                                                     Our Reference
      t   • I I                                                      D/DAS/64/2.-
                                                                    Date
                                                                    + Dece~?er 2001


            I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
    5 November 2001, which was passed to the Ministry of Defence by Scottish Airways. We are the
    focal point within the MOD for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

            First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry ofDefence examines any reports of
    'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
    defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
    might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity.· Unless there is evidence of a
    potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
    to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
    explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
    function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
    expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

            With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
    files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 5 November from
    anywhere in Scotland. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the
    United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

                                                                     Date:          20 Nov 2001


UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECT REPORT- 10 November 2001

Thank you very much for your recent letter reporting the sighting of possible unidentified flying objects
near Stillington on the evening of 10 November.

As RAF Fylingdales is not a flying station, no information is held on military flying exercises that have
taken place. However, your information was signalled to an office within the Ministry of Defence that
deals with such sightings and reports. The task of this office is to establish whether what was seen
might have some defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United
Kingdom's airspace might have been compromised by unauthorised aircraft.

It may be of interest to you that RAF Fylingdales received no other UFO reports on the evening of
10 November, although we note that you quote 2 independent witnesses to the event in your letter.

I notice that you have written previously to report sightings so, once again, thank you for your report
and your continued interest.

        I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
5 November 2001 which was passed to this office by the Aeronautical Information Service
(Military) at RAF West Drayton. We are the focal point within the Ministry of Defence for
correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 5 November from
Surrey. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to suggest that the United
Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

       I am writing with reference to your report of a sighting of an 'unidentified flying object' on
31 October 2001 which was passed to us by RAF Cottesmore. This office is the focal point within
the Ministry ofDefence for correspondence relating to 'UFOs.'

        First, it may be helpful if I explain that the Ministry of Defence examines any reports of
'unidentified flying objects' it receives solely to establish whether what was seen might have some
defence significance; namely, whether there is any evidence that the United Kingdom's airspace
might have been compromised by hostile or unauthorised air activity. Unless there is evidence of a
potential threat, and to date no 'UFO' report has revealed such evidence, MOD does not attempt
to identify the precise nature of each reported sighting. We believe it is possible that rational
explanations, such as aircraft lights or natural phenomena, could be found for them, but it is not the
function of the MOD to provide this kind of aerial identification service. We could not justify
expenditure of public funds on investigations which go beyond our specific defence remit.

        With regard to your particular observation, I have looked back through our sighting report
files and can confirm that we received no other reports of 'UFO' sightings for 31 October from
anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

      from anywhere in the United Kingdom. We are satisfied that there is no corroborating evidence to
      suggest that the United Kingdom's airspace was breached by unauthorised air activity.

MESSAGE LEFT ON DAS ANSWERPHONE

11 October 2001 - 3.20pm


Message:
"You have heard from me before. I am a gifted person with clear vision. I want to
know what is happening because all the batteries of the world are being foreclosed
and the planets are in danger. Jupiter is only 2 miles from the Earth and this is
endangering planes. The Earth, Jupiter and Saturn are in danger. I suggest the
observatories around the world are contacted and they must observe what is
happening because the planets are being endangered."
